10:19 AM

Scrutinized interface screens systematically for professional design deficiencies
Scrutinized interface screens systematically for professional design deficiencies
Looking at all 9 screens carefully as someone who evaluates insurance and fintech interfaces professionally. Here is what is wrong and what is missing.

What Is Actually Good
Splash screen is clean. Login screen is minimal and correct. Color consistency is solid throughout. The ISS circular ring on the dashboard is the strongest single element in the entire app.

The Real Problems — Screen By Screen
Dashboard (Home Screen)
The hierarchy is broken. The ISS score is the most important element but it sits in the middle of the screen competing with the predictive nudge and the policy card. A user's eye lands on "Good morning Ravi" first, then gets lost.

The predictive nudge card (heavy rain expected Friday) is the single most actionable thing on this screen — it needs to be the first thing after the greeting, not buried below the ISS score. The user needs to act on it now. The ISS score is informational — it can wait.

The Quick Actions section at the bottom is cut off. That is a dead end visually. Either show it fully or remove it entirely.

Policy & Plans Screen
This is the most critical screen and it has the most problems.

The plan descriptions are wrong for your product. "Accident & Disability" and "Hospital Cash" appear under Basic Shield and Standard Shield. Those are explicitly excluded by the competition rules. This will get you marked down immediately if a judge sees it.

The Add-on Riders section shows "Vehicle Damage" and "Device Insurance" — both completely outside your scope. Again an instant red flag for Guidewire judges.

The plan cards are too similar visually. Basic, Standard, Full, and Elite look almost identical — same layout, same weight, only the price and a badge differ. A user cannot immediately feel the difference in value between plans. The Elite card should feel significantly more premium than the Basic card.

Claims Screen
"Medical Expense" and "Accident Coverage" and "Gear Damage" appear as claim types. These are completely outside your scope. This is the single biggest problem in the entire app. A judge who sees this will immediately question whether your team understood the brief at all.

Your claims should only show: Income Disruption, Rain Disruption, Platform Downtime, Heat Disruption, Pollution Disruption. Nothing else.

The floating green + button at the bottom right is wrong conceptually. Workers never manually submit claims in your system — claims are auto-generated. Having a + button implies they can create claims manually. Remove it entirely and replace with a text line: "Claims are triggered automatically when disruptions occur."

Wallet Screen
The Recent Transactions section shows "Health Cover — Daily subscription -₹12." Again outside your scope entirely.

The "Zomato Delivery Gig — Completed order #4920 +₹120" transaction is interesting but creates confusion. Your wallet should only show insurance-related transactions — premium deductions and payout credits. Mixing delivery earnings with insurance transactions makes it unclear what this app actually does.

Profile Screen
The bottom navigation shows HOME, GIGS, HISTORY, PROFILE. "GIGS" is not a concept in your app. Your navigation should be HOME, POLICY, CLAIMS, WALLET, PROFILE consistently across all screens.

The ISS chart showing 88% with +5% trend is good. Keep this.

Admin Dashboard
This is actually the strongest screen. The heatmap, fraud queue, and stress test panel are all correct and impressive. The only issue is the Weekly Claims Performance chart shows Approved vs Rejected as the two lines. It should show Premium Collected vs Payout Released — that is the insurer's actual KPI, not approved vs rejected count.

The Deeper Problem Nobody Will Say
Every screen looks like it was designed for a generic insurance app, not specifically for a gig delivery worker using a phone while riding a motorcycle.

A delivery worker checking this app will do it in 3 seconds at a red light. Every key action needs to be reachable with one thumb in the bottom 40% of the screen. The ISS score and the active policy status — the two things they check most — are both in the upper half of the dashboard. They should be in the lower half where the thumb naturally rests.

The font sizes for key numbers (ISS score, wallet balance, payout amount) are correct. But the secondary text is too small for someone reading in sunlight on a moving vehicle. Everything below 14px needs to go up.

The Five Fixes That Matter Most Before Submission
1. Remove every reference to health, accident, medical, vehicle, gear, device from all screens — claims, policy plans, riders, and wallet transactions.

2. Fix bottom navigation to be consistent across all screens — HOME, POLICY, CLAIMS, WALLET, PROFILE.

3. Remove the manual claim + button from the claims screen and replace with auto-generation messaging.

4. Move the predictive nudge card above the ISS score on the dashboard — it is the most urgent action item.

5. Make the policy plan cards visually differentiated — Basic should look basic, Elite should look premium. Right now they look identical with a different price tag.

Fix those five things and the screens go from "looks like a prototype" to "looks like a product." The design language itself is already good — the problems are all content and hierarchy, not aesthetics.

Give the rompt to fix it

inspiration from the Acko insurance app style:

Three things to note after pasting this into Stitch:

The Support screen is the biggest addition here — Acko, Digit, and every serious insurance app has it prominently. Guidewire judges will look for this because support infrastructure is a real insurance business requirement, not just a product feature.

The claim detail screen with Acko-style progress tracker is the second biggest upgrade — this is what makes your demo video cinematic. When you trigger a fake rain event in the Phase 3 demo, judges watch this screen update step by step in real time. That moment needs to look polished.

The floating help button on every screen signals that this is a mature insurance product, not a student hackathon project. That single element changes the perceived quality of everything around it.

10:37 AM

Deliberated sequential screen-by-screen approach over consolidated prompt strategy
Deliberated sequential screen-by-screen approach over consolidated prompt strategy
Setup prompt first, then screen by screen. Here is why — AntiGravity works like a focused agent. One massive prompt loses detail halfway through. Screen by screen with the image uploaded alongside each prompt gives the most accurate replication because the AI sees exactly what to build.

STEP 1 — Paste This Setup Prompt First (No Image Yet)
You are building a Flutter mobile app called ShieldGig.
This is a parametric income insurance app for Zomato 
delivery workers in India.

Set up the complete project foundation before building 
any screens.

- Flutter with Material 3
- GoRouter for navigation
- SharedPreferences for local storage
- fl_chart for all charts
- google_maps_flutter for zone map
- geolocator for location
- firebase_messaging for notifications

How To Use This In AntiGravity
Order to follow:

Step 1 — setup prompt, no image
Steps 2–9 — each with its matching image uploaded alongside
Step 10 — admin dashboard image
Step 11 — no image needed
After each screen is generated:

Test it in the preview
If something is wrong paste: "Fix only this: [describe the specific issue]. Do not change anything else."
Never ask it to redo the whole screen for a small fix
The golden rule for AntiGravity: One screen per session. Do not paste two screen prompts together. It will mix them up.

How To Use All These Prompts
The exact order in AntiGravity:

Paste setup prompt first (no image) — creates project foundation
Upload screen__8_.png → paste Prompt 1 (Splash)
Upload screen__4_.png → paste Prompt 2 (Login)
Upload screen__9_.png → paste Prompt 3 (Onboarding)
Upload screen__1_.png → paste Prompt 4 (Onboarding Complete)
Upload screen__5_.png → paste Prompt 5 (Dashboard)
Upload screen__2_.png → paste Prompt 6 (Policy)
Upload screen.png → paste Prompt 7 (Claims)
Upload screen__7_.png → paste Prompt 8 (Claim Detail)
Upload screen__3_.png → paste Prompt 9 (Wallet)
Upload screen__6_.png → paste Prompt 10 (Support)
Upload screen__10_.png → paste Prompt 11 (Profile)
One critical rule — after each screen is generated, test it before moving to the next. If something is off, paste exactly: "Fix only [specific element]. Match the uploaded image. Do not change anything else." Never regenerate the whole screen for a small fix.

not food deliveery q commerce

12:49 PM
Smart pivot. Q-commerce is actually a stronger persona choice than food delivery for this competition. Here is why and what changes.

Why Q-Commerce Is Better
1. Higher income volatility Zepto/Blinkit/Swiggy Instamart workers do 15-25 deliveries per day in tight hyperlocal zones. A 2-hour rain disruption wipes out more of their daily income proportionally than a food delivery worker because q-commerce peak hours are shorter and more concentrated.

2. Hyperlocal zones are already built into q-commerce Zepto operates in 10-minute delivery radius dark stores. Your hyperlocal zone model maps perfectly to this — each dark store's delivery radius becomes a natural zone. This makes your parametric triggers more precise and more justifiable.

3. Nobody else will pick this Every team will pick Zomato/Swiggy food delivery. Q-commerce workers are underrepresented despite being the fastest growing segment. Judges will notice the differentiation immediately.

4. Platform downtime trigger is more impactful Q-commerce apps go down more frequently than food delivery because they are newer infrastructure. Your platform downtime trigger is more relevant here.

What Changes In Your System
Persona Changes
Old: Ravi, 27, Zomato food delivery partner, Chennai

New: Karthik, 24, Zepto delivery partner, Chennai

Works out of Zepto dark store in Adyar
Does 18-22 deliveries per day
Peak hours: 8-11 AM, 5-9 PM
Average weekly income: ₹3,500-5,000
Works in tight 2-3km radius around dark store
Income drops to zero if dark store closes or app goes down
Zone Model Changes
Q-commerce zones are defined by dark store delivery radius not arbitrary city grids.

Old model:
5km x 5km geographic grid

New model:
Dark store delivery radius zones
Each dark store = one zone
Radius: 2-3km from store location

Chennai dark store locations (Zepto):
- Adyar dark store → Zone CHN-DS-001
- Anna Nagar dark store → Zone CHN-DS-002
- OMR dark store → Zone CHN-DS-003
- Velachery dark store → Zone CHN-DS-004
- T Nagar dark store → Zone CHN-DS-005
This is more accurate than grid zones and shows genuine understanding of how q-commerce operates.

New Disruption Types Specific To Q-Commerce
1. Dark Store Closure If the dark store itself closes (flooding, power cut, fire, local order) — every worker assigned to that store loses 100% income.

Trigger: Dark store operational status = CLOSED
Duration: minimum 2 hours
Payout: 100% of daily income estimate
2. Micro-Zone Flooding Q-commerce workers operate in 2-3km radius. A single flooded underpass or road can make the entire zone unreachable even if it is not raining heavily citywide.

Trigger: Primary access routes to dark store 
         blocked (waterlogging advisory for 
         specific road)
Payout: 65% — zone partially accessible via 
        alternate routes
3. Inventory Freeze When Zepto runs out of inventory at a dark store during high demand, they stop accepting orders. Workers are present and ready but earning nothing.

Trigger: Order volume from specific dark store 
         drops >85% for 90+ minutes during 
         peak hours
Payout: 40% — operational disruption not 
        environmental
4. 10-Minute Promise Breach Events During heavy rain, Zepto suspends the 10-minute guarantee. This causes order volume to drop sharply because customers switch to other platforms.

Trigger: Platform suspends delivery promise 
         AND rainfall >15mm in zone
Compound trigger: higher payout than either 
                  trigger alone
Payout: 70%
Premium Model Changes
Q-commerce workers have different risk profiles than food delivery:

Base weekly income estimate: ₹4,000
(lower than food delivery but more consistent 
per-delivery income)

Peak hours concentration:
Morning: 8-11 AM (3 hours)
Evening: 5-9 PM (4 hours)
Total peak: 7 hours per day

Disruption impact is higher during peak hours
Premium should reflect time-of-day risk:

Time-weighted premium calculation:
If disruption occurs during peak hours → 
  income_impact_coefficient × 1.4
If disruption outside peak → 
  income_impact_coefficient × 0.7
What Stays The Same
Everything else in your system stays identical:

Weekly settlement model
Fraud detection signals
ISS score
AI risk engine
Zone depth scoring
Parametric trigger thresholds for rain/heat/AQI
Flutter app screens
Backend architecture
README Changes Needed
Three sections need updating:

Persona section: Change Ravi/Zomato to Karthik/Zepto Update income figures and working pattern

Zone model section: Change 5km grid to dark store radius model Add dark store location list for Chennai

Disruption model: Add the 4 new q-commerce specific triggers Keep all existing environmental triggers

What To Tell The Insurance Expert
When you do the domain session, frame it around q-commerce specifically:

"We are building parametric income insurance for Zepto q-commerce delivery workers in Chennai. Their working zone is defined by a 2-3km dark store delivery radius. We want to validate whether dark store closure and inventory freeze events qualify as legitimate parametric insurance triggers from an underwriting perspective."

That question alone will make the expert lean forward. Nobody has asked them that before.

Want me to update the full README with q-commerce persona and the new disruption triggers right now?

Core Infrastructure & Navigation
Theming & Styles: Custom color palette, typography styles, and input decorations are all set up centrally to match the design language.
Routing: GoRouter is fully implemented with proper authentication redirection.
Main Shell ScaffoldWithNav: The sticky bottom navigation bar (Home, Policy, Claims, Wallet, Profile) and the floating 'Help' button are implemented and apply across all main app tabs.

Here is one single prompt to paste into AntiGravity that does all three priorities together:

The ShieldGig Flutter app UI is already fully built.

======================

```dart
import 'package:flutter/foundation.dart';

class WorkerModel {
  final String name;
  final String platform;
  final String city;
  final String zone;
  final int issScore;
  final int weeklyIncomeEstimate;

  WorkerModel({
    required this.name,
    required this.platform,
    required this.city,
    required this.zone,
    required this.issScore,
    required this.weeklyIncomeEstimate,
  });
}

class PolicyModel {
  final String plan;
  final int premium;
  final String status;
  final String coverageStart;
  final String coverageEnd;
  final List<String> riders;
  final String coverageDescription;

  PolicyModel({
    required this.plan,
    required this.premium,
    required this.status,
    required this.coverageStart,
    required this.coverageEnd,
    required this.riders,
    required this.coverageDescription,
  });
}

class ClaimModel {
  final String id;
  final String type;
  final String date;
  final int amount;
  String status;
  final String zone;
  final String icon;

  ClaimModel({
    required this.id,
    required this.type,
    required this.date,
    required this.amount,
    required this.status,
    required this.zone,
    required this.icon,
  });
}

class ActiveDisruption {
  final String type;
  final String message;
  final int payoutExpected;
  final String creditDate;
  bool isActive;

  ActiveDisruption({
    required this.type,
    required this.message,
    required this.payoutExpected,
    required this.creditDate,
    required this.isActive,
  });
}

class MockDataService extends ChangeNotifier {

  // WORKER
  WorkerModel worker = WorkerModel(
    name: "Karthik",
    platform: "Zepto",
    city: "Chennai",
    zone: "Adyar Dark Store Zone",
    issScore: 62,
    weeklyIncomeEstimate: 4200,
  );

  // POLICY
  PolicyModel activePolicy = PolicyModel(
    plan: "Standard Shield",
    premium: 72,
    status: "ACTIVE",
    coverageStart: "Mon 17 Mar",
    coverageEnd: "Sun 23 Mar",
    riders: ["App Downtime"],
    coverageDescription: "Rain, heat, pollution, app downtime",
  );

  // WALLET
  int walletBalance = 2340;
  int monthlySavings = 1840;
  int potentialLoss = 2100;

  // CLAIMS
  List<ClaimModel> claims = [
    ClaimModel(
      id: "CLM001",
      type: "Rain Disruption",
      date: "Oct 12, 2023",
      amount: 450,
      status: "APPROVED",
      zone: "Adyar Dark Store Zone",
      icon: "rain",
    ),
    ClaimModel(
      id: "CLM002",
      type: "Platform Downtime",
      date: "Oct 08, 2023",
      amount: 200,
      status: "APPROVED",
      zone: "Adyar Dark Store Zone",
      icon: "downtime",
    ),
    ClaimModel(
      id: "CLM003",
      type: "Extreme Heat",
      date: "Today",
      amount: 300,
      status: "PENDING",
      zone: "Adyar Dark Store Zone",
      icon: "heat",
    ),
  ];

  // ACTIVE DISRUPTION (shown as red banner on dashboard)
  ActiveDisruption? activeDisruption;

  // PREDICTIVE NUDGE (shown as amber card on dashboard)
  bool showPredictiveNudge = true;
  String predictiveMessage = "Heavy rain expected Friday";
  int protectAmount = 800;

  // SHADOW POLICY NUDGE (shown when uninsured)
  bool showShadowNudge = false;
  int missedAmount = 680;

  // ISS HISTORY for chart (6 weeks)
  List<double> issHistory = [55, 60, 52, 68, 58, 62];

  // TRANSACTIONS for wallet
  List<Map<String, dynamic>> transactions = [
    {
      "type": "credit",
      "title": "Rain Disruption Payout",
      "subtitle": "Adyar Dark Store Zone",
      "amount": 595,
      "date": "Oct 12, 2023",
    },
    {
      "type": "debit",
      "title": "Standard Shield Premium",
      "subtitle": "Week of Oct 10, 2023",
      "amount": 72,
      "date": "Oct 10, 2023",
    },
    {
      "type": "credit",
      "title": "Claim-Free Cashback",
      "subtitle": "4 weeks bonus reward",
      "amount": 42,
      "date": "Oct 08, 2023",
    },
    {
      "type": "debit",
      "title": "App Downtime Rider",
      "subtitle": "Week of Oct 10, 2023",
      "amount": 12,
      "date": "Oct 10, 2023",
    },
  ];

  // ============================================
  // DEMO TRIGGER METHODS
  // ============================================

  void triggerRainDisruption() {
    // Step 1: Show active disruption banner
    activeDisruption = ActiveDisruption(
      type: "Rain",
      message: "Rain disruption in your zone",
      payoutExpected: 595,
      creditDate: "Sunday night",
      isActive: true,
    );

    // Step 2: Add new PENDING claim
    claims.insert(0, ClaimModel(
      id: "CLM_DEMO_${DateTime.now().millisecondsSinceEpoch}",
      type: "Rain Disruption",
      date: "Just now",
      amount: 595,
      status: "PENDING",
      zone: "Adyar Dark Store Zone",
      icon: "rain",
    ));

    notifyListeners();

    // Step 3: After 3 seconds move to APPROVED
    Future.delayed(const Duration(seconds: 3), () {
      claims.first.status = "APPROVED";
      walletBalance += 595;
      monthlySavings += 595;
      transactions.insert(0, {
        "type": "credit",
        "title": "Rain Disruption Payout",
        "subtitle": "Auto-triggered — Adyar Dark Store Zone",
        "amount": 595,
        "date": "Just now",
      });
      notifyListeners();
    });
  }

  void triggerPlatformDowntime() {
    activeDisruption = ActiveDisruption(
      type: "Downtime",
      message: "Zepto app downtime in your zone",
      payoutExpected: 280,
      creditDate: "Sunday night",
      isActive: true,
    );

    claims.insert(0, ClaimModel(
      id: "CLM_DEMO_${DateTime.now().millisecondsSinceEpoch}",
      type: "Platform Downtime",
      date: "Just now",
      amount: 280,
      status: "PENDING",
      zone: "Adyar Dark Store Zone",
      icon: "downtime",
    ));

    notifyListeners();

    Future.delayed(const Duration(seconds: 3), () {
      claims.first.status = "APPROVED";
      walletBalance += 280;
      transactions.insert(0, {
        "type": "credit",
        "title": "Platform Downtime Payout",
        "subtitle": "Auto-triggered — Zepto outage detected",
        "amount": 280,
        "date": "Just now",
      });
      notifyListeners();
    });
  }

  void triggerExtremeHeat() {
    activeDisruption = ActiveDisruption(
      type: "Heat",
      message: "Extreme heat alert in your zone",
      payoutExpected: 210,
      creditDate: "Sunday night",
      isActive: true,
    );

    claims.insert(0, ClaimModel(
      id: "CLM_DEMO_${DateTime.now().millisecondsSinceEpoch}",
      type: "Extreme Heat",
      date: "Just now",
      amount: 210,
      status: "PENDING",
      zone: "Adyar Dark Store Zone",
      icon: "heat",
    ));

    notifyListeners();

    Future.delayed(const Duration(seconds: 3), () {
      claims.first.status = "APPROVED";
      walletBalance += 210;
      transactions.insert(0, {
        "type": "credit",
        "title": "Extreme Heat Payout",
        "subtitle": "Auto-triggered — 43°C threshold exceeded",
        "amount": 210,
        "date": "Just now",
      });
      notifyListeners();
    });
  }

  void dismissDisruption() {
    activeDisruption = null;
    notifyListeners();
  }

  void dismissPredictiveNudge() {
    showPredictiveNudge = false;
    notifyListeners();
  }

  void resetDemo() {
    walletBalance = 2340;
    monthlySavings = 1840;
    activeDisruption = null;
    showPredictiveNudge = true;
    claims = [
      ClaimModel(
        id: "CLM001",
        type: "Rain Disruption",
        date: "Oct 12, 2023",
        amount: 450,
        status: "APPROVED",
        zone: "Adyar Dark Store Zone",
        icon: "rain",
      ),
      ClaimModel(
        id: "CLM002",
        type: "Platform Downtime",
        date: "Oct 08, 2023",
        amount: 200,
        status: "APPROVED",
        zone: "Adyar Dark Store Zone",
        icon: "downtime",
      ),
      ClaimModel(
        id: "CLM003",
        type: "Extreme Heat",
        date: "Today",
        amount: 300,
        status: "PENDING",
        zone: "Adyar Dark Store Zone",
        icon: "heat",
      ),
    ];
    transactions = [
      {
        "type": "credit",
        "title": "Rain Disruption Payout",
        "subtitle": "Adyar Dark Store Zone",
        "amount": 595,
        "date": "Oct 12, 2023",
      },
      {
        "type": "debit",
        "title": "Standard Shield Premium",
        "subtitle": "Week of Oct 10, 2023",
        "amount": 72,
        "date": "Oct 10, 2023",
      },
```

======================
TASK 2 — REGISTER SERVICE IN MAIN.DART
======================

In main.dart add MultiProvider wrapping the app:
```dart
import 'package:provider/provider.dart';
import 'services/mock_data_service.dart';

void main() {
  runApp(
    MultiProvider(
      providers: [
        ChangeNotifierProvider(create: (_) => MockDataService()),
      ],
      child: const ShieldGigApp(),
    ),
  );
}
```

Add provider to pubspec.yaml dependencies:
```yaml
dependencies:
  provider: ^6.1.2
```

TASK 3 — ADD DEMO CONTROL PANEL

Create file: lib/widgets/demo_control_panel.dart
```dart
import 'package:flutter/material.dart';
import 'package:provider/provider.dart';
import '../services/mock_data_service.dart';

void showDemoPanel(BuildContext context) {
  showModalBottomSheet(
    context: context,
    backgroundColor: Colors.white,
    shape: const RoundedRectangleBorder(
      borderRadius: BorderRadius.vertical(top: Radius.circular(24)),
    ),
    builder: (context) {
      final mockData = Provider.of<MockDataService>(
        context, listen: false);

      return Padding(
        padding: const EdgeInsets.all(24),
        child: Column(
          mainAxisSize: MainAxisSize.min,
          crossAxisAlignment: CrossAxisAlignment.start,
          children: [
            // Handle bar
            Center(
              child: Container(
                width: 40, height: 4,
                decoration: BoxDecoration(
                  color: Colors.grey[300],
                  borderRadius: BorderRadius.circular(2),
                ),
              ),
            ),
            const SizedBox(height: 16),

            // Title
            const Text(
              "Demo Controls",
              style: TextStyle(
                fontSize: 18,
                fontWeight: FontWeight.bold,
                color: Color(0xFF1A1A2E),
              ),
            ),
            const Text(
              "Internal use only — tap to simulate disruptions",
              style: TextStyle(fontSize: 12, color: Colors.grey),
            ),
            const SizedBox(height: 24),

            // Rain button
            _DemoButton(
              icon: Icons.water_drop,
              label: "Trigger Rain Disruption",
              subtitle: "Simulates 67mm rainfall → ₹595 payout",
              color: const Color(0xFF1976D2),
              bgColor: const Color(0xFFE3F2FD),
              onTap: () {
                Navigator.pop(context);
                mockData.triggerRainDisruption();
                ScaffoldMessenger.of(context).showSnackBar(
                  const SnackBar(
                    content: Text(
                      "🌧 Rain disruption detected — claim processing"),
                    backgroundColor: Color(0xFF1976D2),
                    duration: Duration(seconds: 3),
                  ),
                );
              },
            ),
            const SizedBox(height: 12),

            // Downtime button
            _DemoButton(
              icon: Icons.cloud_off,
              label: "Trigger Platform Downtime",
              subtitle: "Simulates Zepto outage → ₹280 payout",
              color: const Color(0xFF00897B),
              bgColor: const Color(0xFFE0F2F1),
              onTap: () {
                Navigator.pop(context);
                mockData.triggerPlatformDowntime();
                ScaffoldMessenger.of(context).showSnackBar(
                  const SnackBar(
                    content: Text(
                      "📵 Platform downtime detected — claim processing"),
                    backgroundColor: Color(0xFF00897B),
                    duration: Duration(seconds: 3),
                  ),
                );
              },
            ),
            const SizedBox(height: 12),

            // Heat button
            _DemoButton(
              icon: Icons.thermostat,
              label: "Trigger Extreme Heat",
              subtitle: "Simulates 43°C heatwave → ₹210 payout",
              color: const Color(0xFFF57C00),
              bgColor: const Color(0xFFFFF8E1),
              onTap: () {
                Navigator.pop(context);
                mockData.triggerExtremeHeat();
                ScaffoldMessenger.of(context).showSnackBar(
                  const SnackBar(
                    content: Text(
                      "🌡 Extreme heat detected — claim processing"),
                    backgroundColor: Color(0xFFF57C00),
                    duration: Duration(seconds: 3),
                  ),
                );
              },
            ),
            const SizedBox(height: 12),

            // Reset button
            _DemoButton(
              icon: Icons.refresh,
              label: "Reset Demo",
              subtitle: "Restores all data to default state",
              color: Colors.grey,
              bgColor: const Color(0xFFF3F4F6),
              onTap: () {
                Navigator.pop(context);
                mockData.resetDemo();
                ScaffoldMessenger.of(context).showSnackBar(
                  const SnackBar(
                    content: Text("Demo reset to default state"),
                    backgroundColor: Colors.grey,
                  ),
                );
              },
            ),
            const SizedBox(height: 24),
          ],
        ),
      );
    },
  );
}

class _DemoButton extends StatelessWidget {
  final IconData icon;
  final String label;
  final String subtitle;
  final Color color;
  final Color bgColor;
  final VoidCallback onTap;

  const _DemoButton({
    required this.icon,
    required this.label,
    required this.subtitle,
    required this.color,
    required this.bgColor,
    required this.onTap,
  });

  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) {
    return GestureDetector(
      onTap: onTap,
      child: Container(
        padding: const EdgeInsets.all(16),
        decoration: BoxDecoration(
          color: bgColor,
          borderRadius: BorderRadius.circular(12),
          border: Border.all(color: color.withOpacity(0.3)),
        ),
        child: Row(
          children: [
            Container(
              width: 44, height: 44,
              decoration: BoxDecoration(
                color: color.withOpacity(0.15),
                borderRadius: BorderRadius.circular(22),
              ),
              child: Icon(icon, color: color, size: 22),
            ),
            const SizedBox(width: 12),
            Expanded(
              child: Column(
                crossAxisAlignment: CrossAxisAlignment.start,
                children: [
                  Text(label,
                    style: TextStyle(
                      fontWeight: FontWeight.bold,
                      fontSize: 14,
                      color: color,
                    ),
                  ),
                  Text(subtitle,
                    style: const TextStyle(
                      fontSize: 12,
                      color: Colors.grey,
                    ),
                  ),
                ],
              ),
            ),
            Icon(Icons.chevron_right, color: color),
          ],
        ),
      ),
    );
  }
}
```

TASK 5 — CONNECT MOCK DATA TO EXISTING SCREENS
======================

In every existing screen that shows hardcoded data,
replace hardcoded values with Provider data.

DashboardScreen:
```dart
final mockData = Provider.of<MockDataService>(context);

// Replace hardcoded "Karthik" with:
mockData.worker.name

// Replace hardcoded "62" ISS score with:
mockData.worker.issScore

// Replace hardcoded wallet balance with:
mockData.walletBalance

// Show active disruption banner only when:
if (mockData.activeDisruption != null)
  DisruptionBanner(disruption: mockData.activeDisruption!)

// Show predictive nudge only when:
if (mockData.showPredictiveNudge)
  PredictiveNudgeCard(...)
```

ClaimsScreen:
```dart
final mockData = Provider.of<MockDataService>(context);

// Replace hardcoded claim list with:
ListView.builder(
  itemCount: mockData.claims.length,
  itemBuilder: (context, index) {
    final claim = mockData.claims[index];
    return ClaimCard(claim: claim);
  },
)

// Replace hardcoded summary numbers:
// Total claimed = sum of all claim amounts
int totalClaimed = mockData.claims
  .fold(0, (sum, c) => sum + c.amount);

// Total received = sum of APPROVED claims
int totalReceived = mockData.claims
  .where((c) => c.status == "APPROVED")
  .fold(0, (sum, c) => sum + c.amount);

// Pending count
int pendingCount = mockData.claims
  .where((c) => c.status == "PENDING")
  .length;
```

WalletScreen:
```dart
final mockData = Provider.of<MockDataService>(context);

// Balance card
Text("₹${mockData.walletBalance.toString()
  .replaceAllMapped(RegExp(r'(\d{1,3})(?=(\d{3})+(?!\d))'),
  (m) => '${m[1]},')}",
  style: ...
)

// Savings
Text("You saved ₹${mockData.monthlySavings} this month")

// Transactions list
ListView.builder(
  itemCount: mockData.transactions.length,
  itemBuilder: (context, index) {
    final tx = mockData.transactions[index];
    return TransactionRow(transaction: tx);
  },
)
```

ProfileScreen:
```dart
final mockData = Provider.of<MockDataService>(context);

Text(mockData.worker.name)
Text("${mockData.worker.city}, ${mockData.worker.zone}")
Text("${mockData.worker.platform} Delivery Partner")

// ISS chart data
LineChartData(
  spots: mockData.issHistory
    .asMap()
    .entries
    .map((e) => FlSpot(e.key.toDouble(), e.value))
    .toList(),
)
```

6:44 PM
Same root problem. The grid is still splitting the screen in half instead of showing 2x2 cards. This is a web layout issue — on wide screens Flutter's GridView is treating each cell as half the screen width.

The Real Problem
Your app is running in Chrome as a wide desktop browser. The GridView is working correctly for mobile but on desktop each grid cell becomes 50% of the full 1456px screen width — so each card is 728px wide and very tall, causing them to appear one per "row" visually.

Fix Prompt — Paste Into AntiGravity
Fix the support screen grid layout for web/desktop display.
File: lib/screens/support_screen.dart

The grid cards are stretching to half the screen width
on desktop because the screen is too wide.

SOLUTION — Constrain the entire support screen content
to a maximum width of 480px centered on screen.
This makes it look like a mobile app even in browser.

Wrap the SingleChildScrollView content Column in:

Center(
  child: ConstrainedBox(
    constraints: BoxConstraints(maxWidth: 480),
    child: Column(
      crossAxisAlignment: CrossAxisAlignment.start,
      children: [
        // all existing content here
      ],
    ),
  ),
)

Apply this SAME maxWidth: 480 constraint to ALL screens
in the app — not just support screen.

The reason: this app is designed for mobile (375-480px wide)
but running in a 1456px wide Chrome browser window.
Every screen needs this constraint or content will stretch.

Add this constraint to these files:
- lib/screens/support_screen.dart
- lib/screens/dashboard_screen.dart
- lib/screens/claims_screen.dart
- lib/screens/wallet_screen.dart
- lib/screens/profile_screen.dart
- lib/screens/policy_screen.dart
- lib/screens/login_screen.dart
- lib/screens/otp_screen.dart
- lib/screens/onboarding_screen.dart

The easiest way to do this globally is to create a 
reusable wrapper widget:

Create lib/widgets/mobile_container.dart:

class MobileContainer extends StatelessWidget {
  final Widget child;
  const MobileContainer({required this.child, super.key});

  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) {
    return Center(
      child: ConstrainedBox(
        constraints: const BoxConstraints(maxWidth: 480),
        child: child,
      ),
    );
  }
}

Then in every screen wrap the Scaffold body with:
body: MobileContainer(child: SingleChildScrollView(...))

Also fix the duplicate floating help button on support screen:
Remove one of the two green floating buttons.
The support screen should have NO floating help button
since it is already the help screen.
Keep only the headset button on all OTHER screens.
Remove it completely from support_screen.dart.
This single fix solves every layout problem across all screens at once. Once the 480px constraint is applied the app will look exactly like the mobile designs in Chrome.

Run after fix:

flutter run -d chrome
The app should now appear as a centered mobile-width column in the browser. Share a screenshot of the dashboard after this fix.
